对照 · 第 1359 组

Chapter 14

第十四章

对照 · 第 1360 组

When I reached home, I began to weep like a child. There is not a man alive who has not been deceived at least once but does not know what it is to suffer so.

一回到家，我就像个孩子似地哭了起来。只要是受过哪怕一次欺骗的人就不会不知道我有多痛苦。

对照 · 第 1361 组

Weighed down by the kind of fervent resolution which we always think we shall be strong enough to keep, I told myself that I had to put an end to this affair at once, and impatiently waited for morning to come so that I could go and buy a ticket and return to my father and my sister—twin loves on which I could count and which would never let me down.

理智被这种难忍的愤怒打倒，我告诉自己，我必须立刻终结这种爱情，我迫不及待地等待着天亮后去买车票，回到我父亲和妹妹的身边，他们两人的爱可以让我安心依赖，且绝对不会让我失望。

对照 · 第 1362 组

However, I did not want to go away without ensuring that Marguerite knew exactly why I was going. Only a man who is quite out of love with his mistress will leave her without writing.

但是我又不愿就这样离开，我要让玛格丽特清楚地知道我离开她的原因。一个男人只有在不再喜欢他的情人时才会不告而别。

对照 · 第 1363 组

I wrote and rewrote a score of letters in my head.

我反复考虑着应该怎样来写这封信。

对照 · 第 1364 组

I had been dealing with a woman who was like all other kept women; I had poeticized her far too much. She had treated me like a schoo-boy and, to deceive me, had resorted to an insultingly simple ruse—that much was clear. My pride then took over. I had to leave this woman without giving her the satisfaction of knowing how much our parting made me suffer, and this is what I wrote to her, in my most elegant hand and with tears of rage and pain in my eyes: My dear Marguerite, I trust that yesterday's indisposition has not proved too troublesome. I called, at eleven last evening, to ask after you, and was told you had not yet returned. Monsieur de G was altogether more fortunate, for he arrived a few moments later and was still with you at four o'clock this morning.

我所交往的这个女人与其他妓女没有什么区别，之前我把她美化得过头了。她把我当成小学生看，为了欺骗我，她设了一个再简单不过的骗局，这些都是再清楚不过的事实。我的自尊心终于占了上风。我必须离开这个女人，而且绝不能让她知道这次分手带给我的痛苦，我不能让她因此而获得任何快乐。愤怒和痛苦的泪水充满我的眼眶，但我仍用最端正的字体给她写了这封信：亲爱的玛格丽特：我相信您昨天的不适并没有给您带来多大的麻烦。昨天晚上十一点我来问候您，他们告诉我您还没有回来。

对照 · 第 1365 组

原谅我强加给您了一些令人厌烦的时刻，不过请放心，我永远不会忘记您赐予我的那些幸福时光。

对照 · 第 1366 组

对照 · 第 1367 组

Farewell, my dear Marguerite. I am neither rich enough to love you as I should wish, nor poor enough to love you as you would like. Let us both forget: you, a name which must mean very little to you, and I, happiness which has become impossible for me to bear.

再见了，亲爱的玛格丽特。我既没有富有到能像我自己所希望的那样去爱您，也没有贫穷到能完全按照您所要求的那样去爱您。让我们忘记彼此吧：对您来说，是忘记一个本来就没放到心上的名字；对我来说，则是忘记这份无法承受的幸福。

对照 · 第 1369 组

对照 · 第 1370 组

我把这封信反复看了十几遍，想到它会给玛格丽特带来的痛苦，我心里总算有少许的平静。我试图鼓足勇气，装得像信里所写的那样洒脱。八点钟，我的仆人听到召唤来到我的房间，我把信交给他，让他马上送去。

对照 · 第 1375 组

The whole of the time my servant was out, I remained in a state of extreme agitation. At some moments, recalling how completely Marguerite had given herself to me, I asked myself by what right had I written her an impertinent letter when she could quite well reply that it was not Monsieur de G who was deceiving me but I who was deceiving Monsieur de G—which is an argument which allows many a woman to have more than one lover. At other moments, recalling the hussy's solemn oaths, I tried to convince myself that my letter had been far too mild and that there were no words strong enough to scourge a woman who could laugh at love as sincere as mine. Then again, I told myself that it would have been better not to write at all, but to have called on her during the day: in this way, I would have been there to enjoy the tears I made her weep.

在我的仆人去送信的这段时间，我一直处于焦虑不安的状态。有时我会想起玛格丽特是怎样委身于我的，我问自己，究竟有什么权利可以写这样一封无礼的信给她，她可以回答说不是

对照 · 第 1376 组

That night especially did I regret what I had done. I was alone in my apartment, unable to sleep, fretting with worry and jealousy whereas, by letting things take their true course, I should have been at Marguerite' s side hearing her say those sweet words which I had heard on only two occasions, and which now made my ears burn in my loneliness.

那天晚上我对自己的所作所为尤其后悔。我独自一人呆在家里，难以入睡，心中充满焦虑与妒忌；如果我对一切装作毫不知情、任其发展，此刻的我本应在玛格丽特的身边，倾听她缠绵的情话，这些话我只听过两次，对此刻寂寞的我而言，它们越发使我感到耳朵发热。

对照 · 第 1427 组

The most dreadful part of my predicament was that logic put me in the wrong. Indeed, all the indications were that Marguerite loved me. In the first place, there was her scheme for spending a whole summer alone with me in the country. Then there was the plain fact that there was nothing that obliged her to be my mistress, for the money I had was insufficient for her needs or even her whims. So there was nothing more to it, on her part, than the hope of finding sincere affection through me which would be a relief from the mercenary loves which beset her life. And now, on the second day, I was in the process of blighting that hope and repaying with high-handed irony the two nights of love which I had accepted! What I was doing was therefore worse than ridiculous: it was dishonest. Had I simply paid the woman back in order to have the right to pass judgment on her way of life? And did not withdrawing on the second day make me look like some parasite of love who is afraid he is about to be presented with the bill for his dinner? It was extraordinary! I had known Marguerite for thirtysix hours, I had been her lover for twenty-four of them, and was acting like some easily injured party. Far from being only too delighted that she should divide her affections to include me, I wanted to have her all to myself, I wanted to force her, at a stroke, to put an end to the affairs of her past which, of course, represented the income of her future. What cause had I to reproach her? None. She had written to tell me she was unwell when she could easily have said bluntly, with the appalling frankness of some women, that she was expecting a lover; and instead of going along with her letter, instead of taking a walk in any street in Paris except the rue d'Antin, instead of spending the evening with my friends and presenting myself the next day at the time she had indicated, I was behaving like Othello, spying on her, thinking I was punishing her by not seeing her any more. But quite the reverse: she was probably delighted by this separation and must have thought me supremely inane. Her silence was nothing so grand as rancour: it was contempt.

就我现在的处境而言，最糟糕的莫过于理智让我清醒地意识到自己错了。事实上，所有的一切都表明玛格丽特是爱我的。首先，她打算只和我一起去乡下避暑。其次，我没有什么东西可以逼迫她做我的情妇，我的财产供不起她的开销，甚至满足不了她偶尔心血来潮的愿望。因此，毫无疑问，就她而言，她希望能在我身上得到真挚的爱情，她的生活充满了以金钱为目的的爱情，她希望在这种这种真挚的爱情中得到解脱。而我却在第二天就开始要毁掉她的愿望，用无情的嘲讽回敬了她两夜的深情。我的所作所为不但可笑，而且可耻。我从未为这个女人付过一点儿钱，有什么权利去斥责她的生活呢？我在第二天就一走了之，不正像一个情场上的食客，生怕别人拿账单要他付账么？这太荒唐了！我认识玛格丽特才三十六个小时，做她的情人也不过二十四个小时，我却表现得好像我是受害者。她把她的爱情分给我，我非但不感到高兴，还想要独占她的全部，强迫她立刻跟她过去的关系一刀两断，而这些关系，恰恰是她未来的生活来源。我有什么资格责备她呢？没有。她完全可以像那些低俗的女人那样，直截了当地告诉我她要接待另一个情人，但她没有，而是写信对我说她有些不舒服。我没有相信她的话，我没有到安坦街以外的其他巴黎街道散步，我没有跟我的朋友一起消磨这一晚，没有等到第二天约好的时间再与她见面，相反，我却扮演起奥赛罗的角色，监视她的行动，还以为不再去见她就是对她的惩罚。而事实恰好相反，她或许正为这样的分手感到高兴，她一定认为我是个再愚蠢不过的人。她的沉默根本算不上怨恨，只是蔑视而已。

对照 · 第 1428 组

At this point, I should have given Marguerite some present or other which would have left her in no doubt about my liberality and also allowed me, because I had treated her like any other kept woman, to believe that I had no further obligations towards her. But I felt that with the least hint of trade, I should degrade, if not the love she had for me, then at least the love I had for her; and since this love of mine was so pure that it refused to be shared with others, it was incapable of offering a present, however fine, as payment in full for the happiness, however brief, I had been given.

此时此刻，我或许应该送玛格丽特一些礼物或其他什么东西，让她对我的豁达不再怀疑；或许我应该像对待其他妓女那样对待她，这样我就不再觉得欠她任何的情了。但我无法忍受我们的爱情沾上任何的铜臭味，这是对它的亵渎就算不是对她给我的感情的贬低，也是对我给她的感情的玷污；这份爱情是如此纯洁，容不得与他人共享，因此，就算是再贵重的礼物，也无法偿付它所带来的幸福尽管这幸福那么短暂，转瞬即逝。

So the break I had made had altered nothing in Marguerite's habits.

这么看来，我和玛格丽特的分手，并没有对她的生活习惯造成任何的改变。

It is because of moments like this that some people will tell you: "You shouldn't have given the woman another moment's thought. She clearly didn't love you."

这种时候，人们都会对你说：不要再去想这个女人了。她根本就不爱您。

对照 · 第 1457 组

好吧，看到玛格丽特没有因为我而难过，我很高兴。我继续说道，嘴角扯出一个勉强的微笑。

对照 · 第 1458 组

她这样做绝对是正确的。您已经做了您该做的事。您比她理智的多，因为她真的爱过你。她总是把您挂在嘴边，没准儿哪天真会做出什么蠢事来。

对照 · 第 1459 组

'If she loves me, why didn't she reply?'

如果她爱我，为什么不给我写回信呢？

'Because she realized that she was wrong to love you. I know Marguerite; she'd sooner die than give you an answer.'

因为她已经认识到爱您是错的。而且，女人有时会允许别人利用她们的感情，但决不允许别人伤害她们的自尊，尤其是一个人只做了她两天的情人就这样离开她，不管他有怎样的理由，她的自尊心都被深深伤害了。我了解玛格丽特，她是宁死也不会给您回信的。

I resolved that I should suffer no more and leave the next day.

我想我不能再忍受这样的折磨了，决定明天就动身。

下定决心之后，我便开始收拾行李，因为我知道即便躺在床上我也不可能睡着。

Chapter 15

第十五章

对照 · 第 1474 组

Joseph and I had been getting everything ready for my departure for about an hour, when there was a violent ringing at my door.

普鲁登斯在那边站着，盯着我客厅中零星摆放着的几件古董，玛格丽特则坐在沙发上想着什么。我一进去就径直向她走去，跪在她面前握住她的双手，激动地说：原谅我吧。

当然可以。但是一方面我不想麻烦您，另一方面，我敢肯定您到了我家门口就会要求上去坐坐，而我又不能答应您，我可不想因为我的拒绝而使您在离开时又多了一个埋怨我的理由。

对照 · 第 1500 组

对照 · 第 1501–1550 组50 组
对照 · 第 1501 组

'Then why did you deceive me?'

对照 · 第 1502 组

'My dear, if I were the Duchess of This or That, if I had two hundred thousand livres a year, if I were your mistress and had another lover besides you, then you'd have every right to ask why I deceive you. But I am Mademoiselle Marguerite Gautier, I have debts of forty thousand and not a penny behind me, and I spend a hundred thousand francs a year: your question is out of order and my answer irrelevant.'

亲爱的，假如我是某某公爵夫人，假如我有二十万利弗尔的年金，假如我在做您情妇以后又有了其他情人，您尽可以来问我为什么要欺骗您。但我是玛格丽特戈蒂埃小姐，我担负着四万法郎的债务，我没有一点儿财产，我每年还要花掉十万法郎；所以您的问题根本毫无意义，我回答您也只是白费力气。

对照 · 第 1503 组

'You're quite right,' I said letting my head fall on to Marguerite's knees, 'but I do love you, to distraction.'

您说得很对，我把头枕在玛格丽特的膝盖上说，但是我真的爱您，爱得近乎疯狂。

对照 · 第 1504 组

'Well, my dear, you should have loved me a little less or understood me a little better. Your letter hurt me very deeply. If I'd been free to choose, then in the first place I would never have seen the Count the day before yesterday, or, if I had, I would have come to beg you for the forgiveness which you asked of me a few moments ago and, from that moment on, I would have had no other lover but you. There was a moment when I thought I could indulge myself and be really happy for those six months. You would have none of it; you just had to know how I was going to manage it—good heavens! It was easy enough to guess. The sacrifice I was going to have to make if it was to be possible, was much greater than you think. I could have told you: "I need twenty thousand francs." You were in love with me, you would have raised it some how, though there was a risk that one day you'd be sorry you'd done so and blame me. I chose to owe you nothing; you didn't understand my delicacy, for delicacy it is. Girls of my sort, at least those of us who still have some feelings left, take words and things further and deeper than other women. I repeat: coming from Marguerite Gautier, the means which she found of repaying her debts without asking you for the money it took, was an act of great delicacy of which you should now take advantage without another word. If you met me today for the first time, you'd be only too delighted with the promises I'd make you, and you wouldn't ask questions about what I did the day before yesterday. Sometimes, we have no choice but to buy gratifications for the soul at some cost to the body, and it hurts all the more when those gratifications subsequently elude us.'

我亲爱的，您就少爱我一些，或者多了解我一些。您的信让我非常伤心。如果我可以选择，我前天绝不会接待伯爵，就算接待了他，我也会来请求您的原谅，就像刚才您向我请求原谅那样，而且从那刻起，除您之外我不会再有任何情人了。有一阵子我以为自己能放松下来好好享受六个月的快乐日子。但您却不愿意；您非要知道我用的是什么方法，天哪！这难道还用猜吗？我这样做所付出的代价远比您想象的大得多。我本可以对您说：我需要两万法郎。您现在正爱着我，兴许能筹到这笔钱，但以后可能您就会后悔并埋怨我害您这么做。我情愿不欠您任何东西；您不懂得我对您的体贴，因为它太过微妙。我们这样的女人，至少是那些还留有感情的，所说的话、所做的事都有深刻的含义，胜过其他女人。我再对您重复一遍，对玛格丽特戈蒂埃来说，不去向您要钱而自己找办法还清债务是对您的体贴，您应该一声不响地接受。如果您今天才认识我，您会对我答应您的事感到非常高兴，也不会盘问我前天到底干了什么。有些时候为了获取精神上的满足，我们必须付出肉体上的代价，但当精神上的满足也离我们远去之后，我们就会更加痛苦。

对照 · 第 1505 组

'I heard and saw Marguerite with admiration. When I reflected that this marvelous creature, whose feet I once had longed to kiss, should consent to give me a place in her thoughts and a role in her life, and when I thought that I was still not content with what she was giving me, I asked myself whether man's desire has any limits at all if, though satisfied as promptly as mine had been, it can still aspire to something more.

我怀着赞赏的心倾听着玛格丽特的话语，凝望着她的脸庞。我心想，过去我只渴望能亲吻这个绝代佳人的脚，现在她竟让我了解她真实的想法，并且在她的生活中占据了一席之地，而我对此却仍不满足。我不禁自问，人的欲望是否有止境？尽管已经得到满足，我却还想要更多。

对照 · 第 1506 组

'It's true,' she went on, 'we creatures of chance have weird desires and unimaginable passions. Sometimes we give ourselves for one thing, sometimes for another. There are men who could ruin themselves and get nowhere with us; there are others who can have us for a bunch of flowers. Our hearts are capricious: it's their only diversion and their only excuse. I gave myself to you more quickly than I ever did to another man, I swear. Why? Because when you saw me coughing blood, you took me by the land, because you wept, because you are the only human being who ever felt sorry for me. I'm now going to tell you something silly. Once I had a little dog who used to look at we with sad eyes when I coughed: he was the only living creature I have ever loved.

就是这样，她接着说，我们这些受命运摆布的人总会有一些古怪的愿望和不可思议的感情。有些时候我们为一件事情而委身于人，有些时候又是为了其他的事情。有些人为我们倾家荡产，却一无所获；有些人只用一束鲜花就可以拥有我们。我们总是这样心血来潮，这是我们仅有的消遣和借口。我发誓我从没像答应您那么快地答应过其他任何男人。为什么呢？因为那天我咳血的时候，您握住我的手，因为您为我流下了眼泪，因为您是唯一一个真正同情我的人。现在我告诉您一件很可笑的事。从前我有一只小狗，每当我咳嗽的时候，它总会用哀伤的眼神望着我；它是我唯一喜欢过的动物。

对照 · 第 1507 组

'When he died, I cried more than after my mother's death. Mind you, she did spend twelve years of her life beating me. Well, from the start, I loved you as much as my dog. If men only knew what can be had with just one tear, they would be better loved and we should ruin fewer of them.

当它死的时候，我哭得比母亲去世时还要伤心。您要知道，我整整挨了母亲十二年的打骂。就这样，我从一开始就爱上了您，就像我之前爱我的小狗一样。如果男人明白用一滴眼泪可以换到些什么的话，他们就能获得更多的爱，为我们破产的人也会少些。

对照 · 第 1508 组

'Your letter gave you away: it showed me that you didn't understand the workings of the heart, and it injured you more in the love I had for you than anything else you could have done. It was jealousy, of course, but a sarcastic, haughty kind of jealousy. I was feeling miserable when I got the letter. I was counting on seeing you at midday, on having lunch with you, hoping the sight of you would chase away a thought I kept having which, before I knew you, never bothered me in the least.

您的来信暴露了真实的您：它告诉我您并不明白心灵的奥妙所在，在我对您的爱中，它对您的伤害比您能做出的其他任何事情都更大。这当然是嫉妒，但却是可笑而又粗鄙的嫉妒。收到您的信时，我感到万分痛苦。我原指望中午能见到您，和您一起吃午饭，希望见到您我就能抹掉那些一直纠缠着我的想法，而在认识您之前，这些东西我从来都不放在心上。

对照 · 第 1509 组

'Then again,' continued Marguerite, 'you were the only person with whom I'd sensed from the first I could think and speak freely. People who congregate around girls like me can gain a great deal by paying close attention to the slightest words we say, and by drawing conclusions from our most insignificant actions. Naturally, we have no friends, we have egotistical lovers who spend their fortunes not on us, as they claim, but on their vanity.

而且，玛格丽特继续说道，您是唯一一个让我一开始就感觉在您面前可以毫无顾虑、畅所欲言的人。那些喜欢在像我这样的姑娘身边转来转去的人总爱对我们所讲的东西追根究底，想在我们那些最微不足道的举动中找出些所以然。我们自然没有什么朋友，我们有的只是一群自私自利的情人，他们声称为我们挥霍了他们的财产，其实只是为了自己的虚荣心。

对照 · 第 1510 组

For men like these, we have to be cheerful when they are happy, hale and hearty when they decide they want supper, and as cynical as they are. We are not allowed to have feelings, for fear of being jeered at and losing our credibility.

对于这些人，在他们开心的时候，我们也要跟着开心；在他们想吃夜宵的时候，我们也要变得胃口大开；在他们疑神疑鬼的时候，我们也要疑神疑鬼。我们不能有自己的感情，否则就会遭到嘲笑，毁掉自己的名声。

对照 · 第 1511 组

Our lives are no longer our own. We aren't human beings, but things. We rank first in their pride, and last in their good opinion. We have women friends, but they are friends like Prudence—yesterday's kept women who still have expensive tastes which their age prevents them from indulging. So they become our friends, or rather associates. Their friendship may verge on the servile, but it is never disinterested. They'll never give you a piece of advice unless there's money in it. They don't care if we've got ten lovers extra as long as they get a few dresses or a bracelet out of them and can drive about every now and then in our carriages and sit in our boxes at the theatre. They end up with the flowers we were given the night before, and they borrow our Indian shawls. They never do us a good turn, however trifling, without making sure they get paid twice what their trouble was worth. You saw as much yourself the evening Prudence brought me the six thousand francs which I'd asked her to go and beg from the Duke; she borrowed five hundred francs which she'll never give back, or else she'll pay it off in hats that will never get taken out of their boxes.

我们的生命早已不属于自己。我们不再是人，而是物品。当他们想要满足自己的自尊心时，总会先想到我们，但他们却从没正眼看过我们。我们有不少女朋友，但她们都是像普鲁登斯那样的朋友，她们过去也是妓女，习惯了挥霍，但现在人老了，没法再放纵了。于是，她们成了我们的朋友，或者说，合伙人。她们的友情有时甚至就像奴役的关系，但这可不是白做的。除非有钱可赚，否则她们不会给你任何建议。她们不会在乎我们是否有十几个情人，只要能从他们那里搞到几件裙子首饰，或者能经常坐着我们的马车出去逛逛，又或者能坐在我们的包厢里看戏。她们拿走我们前夜用过的花，借用我们的开司米披肩。不管是多么小的事情，如果她们没法得到双倍于她们辛劳的谢礼，她们是绝对不会帮我们忙的。那天晚上您也亲眼看到了，普鲁登斯替我从公爵那要来了六千法郎，却向我借走了五百法郎，那些钱她是永远不会还的，顶多还给我几顶帽子，这也绝不会是她们自己的东西。

对照 · 第 1512 组

So we can have, or rather I had, only one hope of happiness: and this was, sad as I sometimes am and ill as I am always, to find a man of sufficiently rare qualities who would never ask me to account for my actions, and be the lover of my wilder fancies more than the lover of my body . I found this man in the Duke, but the Duke is old and old age neither shields nor consoles. I'd thought I could settle for the life he made for me. But it was no use. I was dying of boredom, and I felt that if I was going to be destroyed, then I might as well jump into the flames as choke on the fumes.

因此我们只能希望，或者说我曾经希望，拥有一种幸福：像我这种偶尔情绪低落，又经常体弱多病的人，这唯一的幸福就是找到具有十分罕见品质的男人，他不过问我的生活，爱我疯狂的想法胜过爱我的身体。我曾经在公爵身上找到过这样的品质，但是公爵已经老了，他既不能保护我，又无法给我安慰。我本以为我能够按照他为我安排的那样生活。但是我做不到。我快要无聊死了，我想如果我要死掉，我宁愿跳到火中烧死，让烟呛死。

对照 · 第 1513 组

Then I met you. You were young, passionate, happy, and I tried to turn you into the man I had cried out for in my crowded but empty life. What I loved in you was not the man you were but the man you could be. You refuse to accept the part; you reject it as unworthy of you; you are a commonplace lover. Just do what the others do: pay me and let's not talk about it any more.'

然后我遇见了您。您是那么年轻、热情、开朗，我试着将您变成我这拥挤却又空虚的生命中迫切想要找到的那个人。我真正爱的，不是现在的您，而是您将会变成的那个人。但您拒绝接受这个角色；您觉得这有失您的身份；您不过是个普通的情人。您还是像别人那样付钱给我吧，我们也别再谈这些事了。

对照 · 第 1514 组

Marguerite, tried by this long confession, settled back into the sofa and, to check a mild fit of coughing, put her handkerchief to her lips and even wiped her eyes.

说完这段长长的表白之后，玛格丽特疲惫地倚在沙发上，她努力忍住阵阵轻微的咳嗽，把手绢按在嘴唇上，甚至擦了擦眼睛。

对照 · 第 1515 组

'Forgive me, forgive me,' I murmured, 'I knew all this, but I wanted to hear you say it, my darling Marguerite. Let's forget the rest. Let's just remember one thing: we belong to one another, we are young and we are in love.

原谅我，原谅我，我喃喃地说，其实这一切我早已明白，但我还是想听您把这些讲出来，我亲爱的玛格丽特。让我们把其他的都忘了吧。我们只需要记住一件事，那就是我们是属于彼此的，我们还很年轻，我们深深相爱。

对照 · 第 1516 组

Marguerite, do with me what you will. I am your slave, your dog. But, in the name of God, tear up the letter I wrote you and don't let me go away tomorrow. It would kill me.'

玛格丽特，随便您怎么处置我都可以。我是您的奴隶，您的小狗。但看在上帝的份上，把我写给您的那封信撕掉吧，明天别让我走。否则我会死的。

'Marguerite withdrew the letter from the bodice of her dress and, as she handed it back to me, said with a smile of infinite sweetness:

对照 · 第 1538 组

Chapter 16

第十六章

对照 · 第 1539 组

I could have told you the start of the affair in a few lines (Armand said to me), but I wanted you to see for yourself the events and stages by which we reached the point where I agreed to everything Marguerite wanted, and Marguerite conceded that she could live only with me.

我本可以把我们感情的开始用三言两语简单带过

对照 · 第 1540 组

It was on the day following the evening when she had come seeking me out that I sent her Manon Lescaut.

就在她来找我那个晚上的第二天，我把《玛农莱斯科》送给了她。

对照 · 第 1541 组

From that moment on, since I could not alter my mistress's way of life, I altered mine. More than anything, I wanted to leave my mind with no time to dwell on the role I had just accepted, for, despite myself, I should have been very unhappy with it. And thus my life, normally so calm, suddenly took on an air of riot and chaos. You must not imagine that the love of a kept woman, however disinterested, costs nothing. Nothing costs more than the constant capricious requests for flowers, boxes at the theatre, supper parties, outings to the country which can never be denied a mistress.

从那以后，因为我无法改变情妇的生活，我选择改变自己的生活。首先，我不给自己任何时间去考虑刚刚接受的新身份，因为一想到这里，我总会不由自主地感到难过。而我的生活也不再像往常那样平静，忽然之间，一切都变得杂乱无章。您别以为既然这个妓女不是因为钱才跟您在一起，她就花不了您多少钱。您无法拒绝情妇对鲜花、包厢、夜宵、郊游这些东西心血来潮的要求，而这些都是很费钱的。

对照 · 第 1542 组

As I have told you, I had no real money of my own. My father was, and still is, the District Collector of Taxes for C. He has a wide reputation for loyal service, thanks to which he was able to raise the money for the surety he had to find before taking up the post. The Collectorship brings in forty thousand francs a year and, during the ten years he has held it, he has paid off his bond and set about putting a dowry for my sister to one side. My father is the most honourable man you could hope to meet. When my mother died, she left an income of six thousand francs which he divided between my sister and myself the day he acquired the appointment for which he had canvassed; then, when I was twenty-one, he added to this small income an annual allowance of five thousand francs, and assured me that I could be very happy in Paris on eight thousand francs if, beside this income, I could establish myself in a position at the bar or in medicine. Accordingly, I came to Paris, read law, was called to the bar and, like any number of young men, put my diploma in my pocket and rather let myself drift along on the carefree life of Paris. My expenses were very modest. However, I regularly got through my year's income in eight months, and spent the four summer months at my father's place, which in all gave me twelve thousand a year and a reputation as a good son. And, moreover, I didn't owe anyone a penny.

正像我之前告诉过您的，我并没有真正属于自己的财产。我的父亲一直是

对照 · 第 1543 组

That was how things stood with me when I met Marguerite.

这就是我认识玛格丽特时的情况。

对照 · 第 1544 组

You will appreciate that, in spite of my wishes, my level of expenditure rose. Marguerite's was a most capricious nature, and she was one of those women who never consider that the countless amusements of which their life is made can be a serious financial drain. As a result, since she wanted to spend as much time with me as possible, she would write me a note in the morning to say that she would have dinner with me, not in her apartment, but in some restaurant either in Paris or in the country. I would collect her, we would dine, go on to the theatre, and often have supper together, and I would spend four or five louis on the evening, which came to two thousand five hundred or three thousand francs a month, which shortened my year to three and a half months, and put me in the position of either having to run up debts or to leave Marguerite.

您可想而知，尽管非我所愿，之后我的开销还是增加了。玛格丽特是个任性的姑娘，她就是那种把各种各样的娱乐当作自己生活一部分，而且根本不在乎这些会带来多少花销的女人。结果，为了尽可能与我多呆些时间，她往往上午就写信约我一起吃晚饭，地点并不是她的家里，而是巴黎或者郊外的一些饭店。我去接她，一起吃晚饭，一起看戏，还经常一起吃夜宵，我一晚上要花掉四五个路易，这样我每月就要有两千五百到三千法郎的开销，一年的收入在三个半月就花光了，我必须借钱，要不就要离开玛格丽特。

对照 · 第 1545 组

Now I was prepared to agree to anything, except the latter possibility.

除了后者，我什么都可以接受。

对照 · 第 1546 组

Forgive me for telling you all this in such detail, but, as you shall see, these circumstances were the cause of the events which follow. The story I tell is true and simple, and I have allowed the unvarnished facts to stand and the onward march of events to emerge unobstructed.

请原谅我说了那么多琐碎的事情，但是您马上就会看到，正是这些琐事，引起了后面的故事。我所讲述的是一个简单而又真实的故事，我只想把原本朴实的情节和事情发展无阻碍地呈现给您。

对照 · 第 1547 组

I realized therefore that, since nothing in the world could weigh heavily enough with me to make me forget my mistress, I should have to find a way of meeting the expense which she forced me to incur. Furthermore, love had run such riot in me that every moment I spent away from Marguerite seemed like a year, and I felt the need to pass those moments through the flame of some passion or other, and to live them so fast that I would not notice that I was living them at all.

我深知，世界上再没有什么东西可以重要到让我忘掉我的爱人，因此，我必须想办法应付我花在她身上的开销。而且，这段爱情早已让我神魂颠倒，只要一离开玛格丽特，我就好像度日如年，我感到自己有必要在某种激情中来打发掉这些时间，让日子快点儿过去，让我忘掉时间的流逝。

对照 · 第 1548 组

I set about borrowing five or six thousand francs against my small capital and began to play the tables, for since the gambling houses were shut down, people have been gambling everywhere. Time was, when you went to Frascati, you stood a chance of winning a fortune: you played against a bank and, if you lost, you had the consolation of telling yourself you might have won. Whereas nowadays, except in the gaming clubs where you still find they are pretty strict about paying up, you can be fairly sure that if you win a large sum you won't see a penny of if. You will readily understand the reasons why.

我以自己小小的资金做抵押着手借来了五六千法郎，开始赌钱了，因为自从赌场被取缔之后，到处都可以赌钱了。以前，人们只要走进弗拉斯卡第赌场，就会有发财的机会：大家都用现钱来赌，如果你输了，你可以安慰自己说本来可以赢的。但现在，除了在俱乐部里付款还比较正规外，在其他地方，就算你赢了一大笔钱，也什么都拿不到。这其中的原因很容易理解。

对照 · 第 1549 组

Gambling is only for young men who have expensive tastes and not enough money to keep up the kind of lives they lead. So they gamble and, in the natural way of things, this is the result: they may win, and then the losers are expected to foot the bill for these gentlemen's horses and mistresses, which is thoroughly disagreeable. Debts are contracted, and friendships begun around the gaming table end in quarrels from which honour and lives invariably emerge somewhat tattered. And if you are a gentleman, you may find you have been ruined by very gentlemanly young men whose only fault was that they did not have two hundred thousand francs a year.

赌博只是那群挥霍无度却又没有足够钱来维持他们所过生活的年轻人的游戏。他们赌钱的结果必然如此：如果他们赢了，那么就由输家替他们的车马和情妇付钱，这是件很不愉快的事。债务渐渐越积越多，那些在赌桌边建立起来的友谊也只能以争吵告终，而这样的争吵总会给名声和生命带来些损伤。如果您是一位绅士，您可能会被另外一群年轻的绅士搞到身败名裂，而他们唯一的错误只不过是没有二十万利弗尔的年金。

对照 · 第 1550 组

There is no need for me to tell you about the ones who cheat. One day, you learn that they have had to go away and that—too late—judgement has been passed on them.

至于那些在赌博中作弊的人，我也不必跟您多说了。总有一天，您会知道他们不得不离开，但那时离开已经太迟了，他们已得到应有的惩罚。

对照 · 第 1551–1600 组50 组
对照 · 第 1551 组

I accordingly threw myself into the fast-moving, bustling, volcanic life which once upon a time had frightened me when I thought of it, and which had now come to be in my eyes the inescapable corollary of my love for Marguerite. What else could I have done?

我因此陷入到一种过去连想都不敢想的紧张、混乱、激烈的生活之中，但现在看来，这只是我爱玛格丽特的必然结果。除此之外，我又能怎么办呢？

对照 · 第 1552 组

During the nights I did not spend in the rue d'Antin, I should not have slept if I had spent them alone in my apartment. Jealousy would have kept me awake and heated my thoughts and blood. On the other hand, gambling temporarily beguiled the fever which would otherwise have overrun my heart which was, thereby, diverted towards a passion fascinating enough to absorb me despite myself until the time came for me to go to my mistress. When that hour struck—and this was how I became aware of how violent my love was —then, whether I was winning or losing, I would abandon the table without compunction, feeling pity for those I left there who, unlike me, would not find happiness when they came to take their leave.

如果我不在安坦街过夜，而是一个人在家里的话，我是绝对睡不着的。嫉妒会燃烧我的思想和血液，让我难以入睡。另一方面，赌博可以暂时让我忘掉那股能击垮我心灵的狂躁，并把它转移到另一种激情上，我不由自主地沉溺其中，直到我终于可以去见我的爱人。到了那个时候，不管是输是赢，我总会毫不留恋地离开赌桌，我为留在那儿的人感到同情，他们不能像我这样，离开时还能那么快乐这正说明了我是多么爱玛格丽特啊！

对照 · 第 1553 组

For most of them, gambling was a necessity; for me, it was a kind of antidote.

对大部分人来说，赌博是一种需要；对我来说，它却是一服良药。

对照 · 第 1554 组

When I was cured for Marguerite, I would be cured of gambling.

如果我不爱玛格丽特，我也不会再去赌博。

对照 · 第 1555 组

And so, in the middle of it all, I was able to keep a fairly cool head. I lost only what I could afford, and won only what I could have afforded to lose.

因此，在赌钱的过程中，我能够一直保持冷静。我只输自己付得起的钱，也只赢自己输得起的钱。

对照 · 第 1556 组

Moreover, luck was on my side. I did not run up debts, and spent three times as much as before I started playing the tables. It was not easy to resist the allurements of a way of life which enabled me to cater for Marguerite's innumerable whims without feeling the pinch. For her part, she still loved me as much, and even more.

而且，我的赌运很好。我没有欠债，但花费却是我赌钱之前的三倍。这种生活方式使我可以轻松地满足玛格丽特各种各样的怪念头，想要抗拒它的诱惑可没那么容易。就她而言，她一直像以前那样爱我，甚至比以前更爱我了。

对照 · 第 1557 组

As I have told you, I began at first by being allowed to stay only between midnight and six in the morning. Then I was allowed into her box at various theatres from time to time. Next, she came and dined with me occasionally. One morning, I did not leave until eight, and there was a day when I did not go until noon.

正如我对您说的，开始的时候她只在夜里十二点到第二天早上六点之间接待我。后来我可以在各个剧院进入她的包厢。再后来她有时还会过来跟我一起吃晚饭。有一天我直到早上八点才离开，还有一天我一直呆到了中午。

对照 · 第 1558 组

Pending her moral transformation, a physical transformation had come over Marguerite. I had undertaken to cure her, and the poor girl, guessing what I was about, did everything I told her as a way of showing her gratitude. Without too much trouble or persuasion, I managed to cut her off almost totally from her old habits. My doctor, whom I had arranged for her to meet, had told me that only rest and quiet could keep her in good health, and consequently, for the supper parties and late nights, I succeeded in substituting a healthy diet and regular sleep. Reluctantly at first, Marguerite took to her new life, the beneficial effects of which she could feel. And soon she began to spend odd evenings at home or, if the weather were fine, she would wrap up well in an Indian shawl, cover her face with a veil, and we would set off on foot, like a couple of children, to roam the evening away along the dusky avenues of the Champs- lys es. She would return weary, take a light supper and retire to bed after playing a little music or reading a few pages, something which had never happened to her before. The coughing fits, which I had found heartrending whenever I heard her racked by them, had almost completely gone.

在期待着玛格丽特发生精神上的转变时，她的身体已经发生了变化。我曾承诺治好她的病，这个可怜的姑娘也猜到了我的心思，乖乖地按照我所说的去做，以表示她对我的感激。没遇到太多困难，也没有做太多劝说，我就让她几乎完全远离了以前的老习惯。我安排我的私人医生帮她看病，他告诉我，只有充足的休息和安静的环境才能让她恢复健康，于是，我帮她戒掉了参加晚宴和熬夜的习惯，而用健康的饮食和规律的睡眠取而代之。虽然开始时并不情愿，但玛格丽特也渐渐适应了这种新的生活，她自已也感到这确实有利于自己的健康。很快她开始在家里度过奇数天的夜晚，如果遇到好天气，就披上一条开司米披肩，拿面纱盖住脸，我们两个就像孩子似的在香榭丽舍大街昏暗的林阴道上散步。她回来的时候会有点疲惫，稍稍吃点夜宵，然后弹会儿琴，或是看会儿书就睡觉了，这样的生活她过去是从来未曾有过的。从前那种我一听就觉得心痛的折磨她的咳嗽声几乎完全消失了。

对照 · 第 1559 组

Within six weeks, there was no further mention of the Count who had been permanently sacrificed. There remained only the Duke to compel me to hide my affair with Marguerite, and even he had often been sent away in my presence on the pretext that Madame was asleep and had left orders that she was not to be disturbed.

过了六个星期，伯爵已经不再被提起，他从我们的生活中永远地消失了。但是，对公爵我还不得不继续隐瞒我和玛格丽特的关系，尽管如此，当我在玛格丽特那里时，公爵还是经常会被夫人还在睡觉，不能被打扰这样的借口打发走。

对照 · 第 1560 组

As a direct result of the habit of seeing me—or rather the need to see me —which Marguerite had contracted, I abandoned gambling at the precise moment when an experienced gambler would also have given up. All in all, with what I had won, I found myself in possession of twelve thousand francs which seemed an inexhaustible capital to me.

玛格丽特养成了见我的习惯，或者更应该说是需要，这直接导致我总能像个高明的赌徒一样，在最该放弃的时候离开赌场。总之，因为不断赢钱，我发现手里已经有了一万两千多法郎，这笔钱对我来说似乎是笔取之不尽的财产。

对照 · 第 1561 组

The time of year had come round when I normally went off to join my father and my sister, and still I did not go. As a result, I received frequent letters from both of them asking me to come and stay with them.

到了一年中我通常去探望父亲和妹妹的时间了，但是我没有去。因此，我经常受到他们两个的来信，催我回家陪一陪他们。

对照 · 第 1562 组

To all their entreaties, I answered as best I could, repeating that I was well and that I was not short of money, two considerations which, I believed, would go some way to consoling my father for delaying the start of my annual visit.

我尽量用最婉转的方式答复他们的请求，我总说我身体很好，也不缺钱，我相信这两点能让因为我迟迟不回家而失望的父亲得到些许的安慰。

对照 · 第 1563 组

Meantime, it came about one morning that Marguerite, who had been woken up by bright sunshine, leaped out of bed and asked me if I would like to take her out to the country for the day.

在这期间，有天早上，玛格丽特被灿烂的阳光照醒了，她跳下床来问我愿不愿意带她去乡下玩一天。

对照 · 第 1564 组

Prudence was sent for and the three of us set out, after Marguerite had left orders with Nanine to tell the Duke that she had wanted to make the most of the weather and had gone to the country with Madame Duvernoy.

我们派人把普鲁登斯请来，玛格丽特又嘱咐纳尼娜告诉公爵，她要趁着这好天气跟迪韦努瓦太太去乡下玩，然后我们三个就一块出发了。

对照 · 第 1565 组

Apart from the fact that the presence of la Duvernoy was necessary to set the old Duke's mind at rest, Prudence was the sort of woman who seems expressly cut out for country outings. With her unquenchable high spirits and insatiable appetite, she was quite incapable of allowing anyone she was with to be bored for an instant, and was more than likely to be an old hand at ordering the eggs, cherries, milk, saut d rabbit and all the usual ingredients of the traditional lunch for which the countryside around Paris is known.

迪韦努瓦太太的陪伴是为了让老公爵放心，但除去这点，普鲁登斯似乎是个天生就适合郊游的人。她似乎有着用不完的精力和永远填不满的胃口，有她做伴，你片刻也不会感到无聊，而且她特别擅长去采购鸡蛋、樱桃、牛奶、煎兔肉还有其他所有巴黎郊游必备的传统食物。

对照 · 第 1566 组

All that remained was to decide where we should go.

接下来只剩下去哪儿的问题了。

对照 · 第 1567 组

Once again, it was Prudence who got us out of this difficulty.

这次又是普鲁登斯帮我们解决了这个难题。

对照 · 第 1568 组

'Is it the real country you want to go to?' she asked.

你们是不是想到真正的乡下去啊？她问我们。

对照 · 第 1569 组

'Yes.'

是的。

对照 · 第 1570 组

'Well, let's go to Bougival, to the Point du Jour. It's run by a widow named Arnould. Arnould, go and hire a barouche.'

那么，我们就去布吉瓦尔好了，去曙光饭店。它是一个叫阿尔努的寡妇开的。阿尔芒，去租一辆四轮马车来吧。

对照 · 第 1571 组

An hour and half later we were in the establishment run by the widow Arnould.

一个半小时以后，我们到了阿尔努寡妇的饭店。

对照 · 第 1572 组

Perhaps you know the inn I mean: it is a hotel during the week and pleasure garden on Sundays. From the garden, which is raised and stands as high as an ordinary first floor, you get a magnificent view. On the left, the Marly aqueduct commands the horizon; on the right, the view unfolds across a never-ending succession of hills; the river, which at this point hardly moves at all, stretches away like a wide ribbon of shimmering white silk between the plain of Les Gabillons and the le de Croissy, and is rocked ceaselessly by the whisper of its tall poplars and the soughing of its willows.

或许您也知道这个饭店，它周一到周六都是宾馆，周日则是一家休闲咖啡馆。它那儿的花园有普通的二层楼那么高，从那里往外看，风景非常优美。左边是一望无际的马利引水渠，右边是连绵不断的小山丘；在这一带几乎停滞的河流，像一条宽阔的闪烁着的银白色缎带，在加比永平原和克鲁瓦西岛之间伸展着，两岸高大的杨树在随风摇曳，柳树在喃喃细语，不停地哄着小河入睡。

对照 · 第 1573 组

Far off, picked out in a wide swathe of sunlight, rise small white houses with red roofs, and factories which, shorn by distance of their grim, commercial character, complete the landscape in the most admirable way.

在稍远一点儿的地方，一大片阳光下显眼地矗立着许多红瓦白墙的小房子和几座工厂，遥远的距离抹掉了那些工厂暗淡枯燥的商业气息，把这周围的景色装饰得更加美好了。

对照 · 第 1574 组

And, far off, Paris shrouded in smoke!

向最远处望去，是烟雾笼罩下的巴黎。

对照 · 第 1575 组

As Prudence had told us, it was really the country and, I must say, it was a real lunch we had.

就像普鲁登斯说的，这里是真正的乡间，另外值得一提的是，我们享用了一顿真正的午餐。

对照 · 第 1576 组

It is not out of gratitude for the happiness I have to thank the place for that I'm saying all this. Bougival, in spite of its unattractive name, is one of the prettiest spots you could possibly imagine. I have travelled a great deal and seen great sights, but none more charming than this tiny village cheerfully nestling at the foot of the hill which shelters it.

我必须感谢这个地方给我带来的快乐，但并不是出于对此的感激我才要这样说的。布吉瓦尔，尽管它的名字不怎么吸引人，但这里确实是您所能想象的最美的地方之一。我之前去过不少地方，见过很多壮丽的景色，但没有哪里比这个恬静地坐落在山脚下的小村庄更迷人的了。

对照 · 第 1577 组

Madame Arnould offered to arrange for us to take a boat out on the river, and Marguerite and Prudence accepted with alacrity.

阿尔努夫人提议安排我们去泛舟游河，玛格丽特和普鲁登斯高兴地接受了。

对照 · 第 1578 组

The countryside has always been associated with love, and rightly so. Nothing creates a more fitting backdrop to the woman you love than the blue sky, the fragrances, the flowers, the breezes, the solitary splendour of fields and woods. However much you love a woman, however much you trust her, however sure of the future her past life makes you, you are always jealous to some degree. If you have ever been in love, really in love, you must have experienced this need to shut out the world and isolate the person through whom you wished to live your whole life. It is as though the woman you love, however indifferent she may be to her surroundings, loses something of her savour and consistency when she comes into contact with men and things. Now I experienced this more intensely than any other man. Mine was no ordinary love; I was as much in love as mortal creature can be. But I loved Marguerite Gautier, which is to say that in Paris, at every turn, I might stumble across some man who had already been her lover, or would be the next day. Whereas, in the country, surrounded by people we had never seen before who paid no attention to us, surrounded by nature in all her springtime finery, which is her annual gesture of forgiveness, and far from the bustle of the city, I could shelter my love from prying eyes, and love without shame or fear.

人们总喜欢把乡村和爱情联系到一起，这是很有道理的。没有什么比这蓝天、芳香、鲜花、微风，还有田野树林的寂静光辉更适合充当您心爱女人的背景了。无论您多爱一个女人，无论您多么信任她，无论她过去的行为使您对未来多么确信，有些时候您还是会嫉妒。如果您曾经恋爱过，我是说那种真正的恋爱，您一定体会过这样一种感觉，那就是想要把那个您想与之过一生的人与世隔绝。这就好像不管您心爱的女人对周围的一切有多冷漠，但只要她一跟其他男人或其他事物接触，她原本拥有的芳香和完整就会消失。这一点我的体会比别人都要深刻。我的爱情是不同寻常的；我的爱到了一个人所能爱的极限。但是我爱的是玛格丽特戈蒂埃，也就是说在巴黎的每个转角我都能碰到一个她以前的情人，或者一个她未来的情人。但是在乡下，我们周围只是些从未遇到过、也不会注意我们的人，我们被大自然用春天的盛装包围，这是大自然一年一度展现它宽容一面的时刻；远离城市的喧嚣，我可以避开那些窥探的目光，不带羞耻、不带顾虑地放手去爱。

对照 · 第 1579 组

There, the courtesan faded imperceptibly. At my side, I had a young and beautiful woman whom I loved, by whom I was loved and whose name was Marguerite: the shapes of the past dissolved and the future was free of clouds. The sun shone on my mistress as brightly as it would have shone on the purest fianc e. Together we strolled through delightful glades which seemed as though they were deliberately designed to remind you of lines by Lamartine and make you hum tunes by Scudo. Marguerite was wearing a white dress. She leaned on my arm. Beneath the starry evening sky, she repeated the words she had said to me the previous night, and in the distance the world went on turning without casting its staining shadow over the happy picture of our youth and love.

在这里，妓女的身份渐渐淡去。我身边是一个名叫玛格丽特的年轻漂亮的姑娘，我深爱着她，她也同样爱着我，过去的一切都已散去，我们的未来一片光明。阳光明亮地照耀在我情人身上，如同照耀在最纯洁的未婚妻身上那样。我们一起漫步在这美丽的地方，它们像是故意被设计成这样，来让人想起拉马丁的诗句，哼起斯居杜的歌曲。玛格丽特穿着一条白色的裙子。她倚在我的手臂上。在灿烂的星空下，她向我重复着前一晚对我说过的话；远处，喧嚣的生活仍在继续，但它无法在我们青春和爱情绘成的快乐画面上投下任何杂色或阴影。

对照 · 第 1580 组

Such was the dream which that day's burning sun brought me through the leafy trees and I, lying full-length in the grass of the island where we had landed, free of all human ties which had hitherto bound me, allowed my mind to run free and gather up all the hopes it met with.

这就是那天的温热阳光透过层层树林给我带来的梦境；我们把船停到一个小岛上，我伸展全身躺在草地上，把之前束缚我的人世间的种种关系都抛到一旁，任由自己的思绪飞扬，把想到的所有希望都收进心里。

对照 · 第 1581 组

Add to this that, from the spot where I lay, I could see, on the bank, a charming little two-storied house which crouched behind a railing in the shape of a semi-circle. Beyond the railing, in front of the house was a green lawn as smooth as velvet, and, behind the building, a small wood full of mysterious hideaways, where each morning all traces of the previous evening's passage would surely be all mossed over.

从我所在的地方，还能看到岸边的一座精致的三层小楼，外面有一道半圆形的栅栏。透过栅栏看，房子的前面有一片天鹅绒般平整的绿草坪，在房子后面还有一片神秘幽静的小树林，那里面前一天踩出的小路，第二天早上就都被新长出来的苔藓盖住了。

对照 · 第 1582 组

Climbing flowers hid the steps leading up to the door of this empty house, and hugged it as far up as the first floor.

一些蔓生植物的花朵铺满了这座空房子的台阶，直到二楼。

对照 · 第 1583 组

Gazing long and hard at the house, I convinced myself in the end that it belonged to me, so completely did it enshrine the dream I was dreaming. I could picture Marguerite and me there together, by day walking in the wood which clothed the hill and, in the evenings, sitting on the lawn, and I wondered to myself if earthly creatures could ever be as happy as we two should be.

我长久地深深凝视着这栋房子，最后竟认为它是属于我的，它是多么符合我刚才的梦境啊。我可以想象出我和玛格丽特住在这里的情景，白天在小山上的树林中散步，晚上一起坐在草坪上；我暗暗想，这世上还有什么人能像我们这样幸福吗？

对照 · 第 1584 组

What a pretty house!' said Marguerite, who had been following the direction of my eyes and perhaps my thoughts.

多漂亮的房子！玛格丽特说，她已经顺着我的目光看到了这座房子，可能也已经知道我在想什么了。

对照 · 第 1585 组

'Where?' said Prudence.

在哪里？普鲁登斯问道。

对照 · 第 1586 组

'Over there.' And Marguerite pointed to the house in question.

在那边。玛格丽特指了指那栋房子。

对照 · 第 1587 组

'Oh, it's lovely,' replied Prudence. Do you like it?'

啊，真漂亮！普鲁登斯回答说。您喜欢它吗？

对照 · 第 1588 组

'Very much.'

非常喜欢。

对照 · 第 1589 组

'Well, then, tell the Duke to rent it for you. He'll rent it for you all right, I'm sure of it. You can leave it all to me if you want.'

那告诉公爵，让他帮您把它租下来吧。我敢肯定他一定会同意的。如果您同意的话，这件事就让我来帮您办。

对照 · 第 1590 组

Marguerite looked at me, as though to ask what I thought of the suggestion.

玛格丽特看看我，似乎在征求我对这个建议的看法。

对照 · 第 1591 组

My dream had been shattered with these last words of Prudence, and its going had brought me back to reality with such a jolt that I was still dazed by the shock.

我的幻想已经随着普鲁登斯的最后几句话破灭了，我被猛然拉回了现实中，这个打击使我头晕目眩。

对照 · 第 1592 组

'Why, it's an excellent idea,' I stammered, not knowing what I was saying.

嗯，这真是个好主意。我结结巴巴地说，根本不知道自己在说些什么。

对照 · 第 1593 组

;In that case, I'll arrange it,' said Marguerite, squeezing my hand and interpreting my words according to her desires. Let's go this minute and see if it's to let.'

那么，一切就由我来安排了。玛格丽特紧握着我的手说，她是按照自己的想法来理解我的话的，我们快去看看它是不是出租。

对照 · 第 1594 组

The house was empty, and to let for two thousand francs.

房子空着，租金是两千法郎。

对照 · 第 1595 组

'Will you be happy here?' she said to me.

在这里你会开心吗？她问我说。

对照 · 第 1596 组

'Can I be sure of ever being here?'

你确定我可以到这里来？

对照 · 第 1597 组

'Who would I choose to bury myself here for, if not for you?'

如果不是为了你，我会为谁躲到这儿来呢？

对照 · 第 1598 组

'Listen, Marguerite, let me rent the house myself.'

听着，玛格丽特，让我自己来租这栋房子吧。

对照 · 第 1599 组

'You must be mad! It's not only unnecessary, it would be dangerous. You know perfectly well that I can only take money from one man. So don't be difficult, silly boy, and don't say another word.'

你疯了吧！这样做不但没有必要，而且还很危险。你清楚地知道我只能从一个男人那里拿钱。所以就别再找麻烦了，傻孩子，别再说了。

对照 · 第 1600 组

'This way, when I've got a couple of days free, I can come down and spend them with you,' said Prudence.

这样的话，如果我有几天的空闲时间，我就过来跟你们一起住。普鲁登斯说。

对照 · 第 1601–1650 组50 组
对照 · 第 1601 组

We left the house and set off back to Paris talking of this latest decision. I held Marguerite in my arms and, by the time we stepped out of the carriage, I was beginning to view my mistress's scheme with a less scrupulous eye.

我们离开了这栋房子，启程回巴黎了，一路上还在谈论这个新决定。我把玛格丽特搂在怀里，等到下马车的时候，我终于能不那么心存顾虑地考虑我的情人的计划了。

对照 · 第 1602 组

Chapter 17

第十七章

对照 · 第 1603 组

The next day, Marguerite sent me away punctually, saying that the Duke was expected early that morning, and promising to write the moment he left to let me know where we should meet in the evening.

第二天，玛格丽特就准时把我打发走了，说公爵很早就会过来，并答应我只要公爵一走就写信通知我晚上约会的地点。

对照 · 第 1604 组

Accordingly, during the day, I received this note: Am going to Bougival with the Duke. Be at Prudence's this evening at eight.'

果然，我在白天就收到了信，信上说：我和公爵一起到布吉瓦尔去了。晚上八点到普鲁登斯家里等我。

对照 · 第 1605 组

At the appointed time, Marguerite was back and she came to meet me at Madame Duvernoy's.

玛格丽特在约定的时间回来了，并到迪韦努瓦太太家见我。

对照 · 第 1606 组

'Well, it's all arranged,' she said as she came in.

行了，一切都安排好了。她一进来就说。

对照 · 第 1607 组

'The house is taken?' asked Prudence.

房子租下来了吗？普鲁登斯问道。

对照 · 第 1608 组

'Yes. He agreed at once.'

是啊。我一说他就同意了。

对照 · 第 1609 组

I did not know the Duke, but I was ashamed to be deceiving him like this.

虽然我不认识公爵，但这样欺骗他，我还是感到很羞耻。

对照 · 第 1610 组

'But that's not all,' Marguerite went on.

不过还不止这些。玛格丽特接着说。

对照 · 第 1611 组

'There's more?'

还有其他事？

对照 · 第 1612 组

'I was worried about where Armand could stay.'

我在考虑阿尔芒的住处。

对照 · 第 1613 组

'Not in the same house?' asked Prudence with a laugh.

他不跟您住在一起吗？普鲁登斯笑着问道。

对照 · 第 1614 组

'No, at the Point du Jour, where the Duke and I had lunch. While he was looking at the view, I asked Madame Arnould—she is called Madame Arnould, isn' t she? —I asked her if she had any suitable apartments. And she has one, with a drawing-room, a reception room and a bedroom. That's all we need, I'd say. Sixty francs a month. The whole place furnished in a manner that would take a hypochondriac's mind off his ailments. I took it. Did I do well?'

不，他住在曙光饭店，我和公爵一起在那里吃的午饭。趁他在看风景的时候，我问过阿尔努太太是阿尔努太太没错吧？我问她那有没有合适的房间可以出租。她那正好有一套，带客厅、会客室和卧室。我敢说我们需要的都有了。租金是六十法郎一个月。那屋里的陈设能让抑郁症患者都变得开心起来。我租下了那套房间。我做得不错吧？

对照 · 第 1615 组

I flung my arms around Marguerite's neck.

我紧紧搂住玛格丽特的脖子。

对照 · 第 1616 组

'It'll be lovely,' she went on. You'll have a key to the side door, and I promised the Duke that he shall have a key to the main gate which he won't take since he'll only ever come during the day when he comes at all. Between ourselves, I think he's delighted by this whim of mine, for it'll get me out of Paris for a while and help to shut his family up. Even so, he did ask how it was that I, who love Paris so much, could make up my mind to bury myself in the country. I told him I wasn't well and this way I could rest. He didn't seem to believe me altogether. The poor old thing always seems to have his back against a wall. So we will be very careful, dear Armand, because he'll have me watched there. And he's not done with just renting a house for me: he's also going to have to pay my debts and, unfortunately, I've a few of those. Is all this all right with you?'

这可太妙了。她继续说。你拿好侧门的钥匙，我答应要把大门的钥匙给公爵，不过他不会要的，因为他即使来也只是白天才来。不要跟别人说，我觉得他对我这个心血来潮的想法很高兴，因为这会让我离开巴黎一些日子，也能让他的家人少说点儿闲话。但他还是问我，我那么喜欢巴黎，怎么会决定自己藏到乡下去。我告诉他说我的身体不太好，在乡下我可以得到休息。他似乎并不完全相信我的话。这个可怜的老头儿似乎总让自己没有退路。所以我们要多加小心，我亲爱的阿尔芒，因为他会派人在那儿盯着我的。他不单只帮我租了一栋房子，而且还要替我还债，因为倒霉的是，我还欠着一些债。你对这样的安排还满意吗？

对照 · 第 1617 组

'Yes,' I replied, trying to silence the scruples which this kind of life awakened from time to time.

满意。我回答说，尽管对这种生活常常感到良心不安，但我还是尽力不表现出来。

对照 · 第 1618 组

We went over the house from top to bottom, and it will be just perfect for us. The Duke fussed over everything. Ah, my dear,' she added, kissing me like a mad thing, 'you can't complain, you've got a millionaire to make your bed for you.'

我们仔细地察看过了房子的每个角落，将来我们在那儿肯定会住得非常开心。公爵把一切都打点好了。啊，亲爱的，她疯狂地吻着我，对我说，你可没什么可抱怨的，有一个百万富翁在为你铺床呢。

对照 · 第 1619 组

'And when are you thinking of moving down there?' asked Prudence.

那您打算什么时候搬过去？普鲁登斯问道。

对照 · 第 1620 组

'As soon as possible.'

越快越好。

对照 · 第 1621 组

'Will you be taking your carriage and the horses?'

您要把马车和马匹也带去吗？

对照 · 第 1622 组

'I shall be taking everything. You can look after the apartment while I'm away.'

我要把所有东西都搬过去。我不在时麻烦您替我看家。

对照 · 第 1623 组

A week later, Marguerite had taken possession of the house in the country and I was installed at the Point du Jour.

一周之后，玛格丽特搬到了乡下那栋房子里，我也住进了曙光饭店。

对照 · 第 1624 组

And so began a life which I could hardly attempt to describe to you.

从那之后，我们便开始了一段很难向您描述的生活。

对照 · 第 1625 组

In the early days of her stay at Bougival, Marguerite was unable to make a complete break with her old ways and, since the house was always in a party mood, all her girlfriends came down to see her. A month went by without a single day when Marguerite did not have eight or ten people sitting round her table. For her part, Prudence invited along everybody she knew and did all the honours of the house, as though the place belonged to her.

刚住到布吉瓦尔的时候，玛格丽特还不能完全改掉她的老习惯，每天家里都像过节一样，所有的女朋友都来看她。一个月过去了，每天都有八到十个人来她家吃饭。普鲁登斯也把她的朋友都请来了，还尽地主之谊带他们参观房子，就好像这是她自己的一样。

对照 · 第 1626 组

The Duke's money paid for it all, as you will have gathered, yet even so Prudence was apt to ask me, from time to time, for the odd thousand-franc note, saying that it was for Marguerite. As you know, I had won some money at the gaming table. So I promptly handed over to Prudence what Marguerite, through her, had asked me for, and, fearing that she might need more than I had, I travelled up to Paris where I borrowed the equivalent of the sum of money which I had borrowed before and had repaid in full.

就像您所想的，所有的一切都是公爵付的钱，但普鲁登斯却不时以玛格丽特的名义向我要张一千法郎的钞票。您知道我之前赌钱时曾赢了些钱。所以我马上把玛格丽特通过她向我要的钱给了她；我害怕我的钱还不够，又到巴黎借了一笔钱，跟之前借过的那笔一样多，当然那笔钱我已经还清了。

对照 · 第 1627 组

I thus found myself rich once more to the tune of ten thousand francs or so, in addition to my allowance.

这样不算我的津贴，我发现我又一次变得有钱，有了一万左右的法郎。

对照 · 第 1628 组

However, the pleasure Marguerite derived from playing host to her women friends slackened off somewhat in view of the expense it involved, and especially in view of the fact that she was on occasion forced to ask me for money. The Duke, who had leased the house so that Marguerite could rest, stopped coming altogether, fearing as always that he would run into a large and high-spirited gathering of people by whom he had no wish to be seen. The reason largely for this was that, turning up one day for a private dinner with Marguerite, he had wandered into the middle of a luncheon party for fifteen which was still going on at a time when he had imagined he would be sitting down to his dinner. When, all unsuspecting, he had opened the dining-room door, his entrance had been greeted by a burst of laughter, and he had been obliged to withdraw hurriedly in the face of the withering glee of the girls who were there.

然而，玛格丽特招待朋友的兴致却慢慢低落，因为她看到这种招待花费巨大，尤其有些时候她还不得不向我要钱。公爵把这栋房子租下来让玛格丽特休养，自己却不再过来，他总是害怕会在这碰到那一大群热闹的客人，他可不想碰到他们。尤其因为有一天他过来跟玛格丽特一起吃晚餐，却碰到十五个人在那参加午宴，午宴在他觉得应该坐下来吃晚餐的时候还在进行。当他打开大门时，怎么也料想不到，迎接他的是一阵疯狂的大笑，在那些姑娘肆无忌惮的欢笑声中，他不得不匆匆离开。

对照 · 第 1629 组

Marguerite had left the table, caught up with the Duke in the next room and had done everything she could to make him overlook the incident. But the old man's pride had been wounded, and he had taken umbrage: he had told the poor girl quite cruelly that he was tired of footing the bill for the follies of a woman who could not even ensure that he was respected under her roof, and he had left very angry.

玛格丽特离开餐桌，在隔壁房间找到了公爵，想尽办法想让他忘掉刚才的场面。但这个老人的自尊心已经受到伤害，他非常恼火：他冷酷地对这个可怜的姑娘说，他不会再为她的荒唐行为买单，她甚至不能确保他能在她那里得到尊重，然后就满脸怒气地走了。

对照 · 第 1630 组

From that day on, we heard nothing more of him. Marguerite sent her guests away and changed her ways, but it did no good: the Duke did not contact her thereafter. I had gained thereby, for my mistress now belonged to me more completely, and my dream was at last coming true. Marguerite could no longer live without me. Without worrying her head about the consequence, she flaunted our affair publicly, and I reached the point where I never left her house. The servants called me 'sir' and regarded me officially as their master.

从那天起，我们就再没听到过他的消息。玛格丽特把她的客人都送走了，也改变了原来的习惯，但还是没用，公爵再也没联系过她。我倒因此获益，因为我的情人现在更加彻底地属于我了，我的梦想终于实现了。玛格丽特再也离不开我。她全然不顾后果如何，公开宣布了我们的关系，而我则到了不离开她家的地步。仆人们叫我先生，正式把我当作了他们的主人。

对照 · 第 1631 组

Of course, Prudence had lectured Marguerite about her new life very sternly, but Marguerite had replied that she loved me, could not live without me and, however it all turned out, would not forgo the joy of having me constantly at her side. And she added that anyone who did not like it was perfectly free to stay away.

当然，对于玛格丽特的新生活，普鲁登斯曾严厉地责备过她，但玛格丽特回答说她非常爱我，不可能离开我，不管有什么后果，她都不会放弃有我在她身边的幸福。她还说

对照 · 第 1632 组

I had heard this for myself one day when Prudence told Marguerite that she had something very important to say to her, and I had listened at the door of the bedroom in which they had closeted themselves.

这些话是我亲耳听到的，有天普鲁登斯来找玛格丽特说有重要的话要跟她谈，我在卧室门外听到了她们在里面的窃窃私语。

对照 · 第 1633 组

Some days later, Prudence came down to see us again.

过了几天，普鲁登斯又来看我们了。

对照 · 第 1634 组

I was at the bottom of the garden when she arrived. She did not see me. Judging by the way Marguerite had gone to meet her, I suspected that another conversation like the one I had already overheard was about to take place, and I was no less anxious to hear what was said.

她进来的时候，我正在花园最里面。她没有看到我。从玛格丽特迎接她的样子判断，我怀疑又要开始一次像上次那样的交谈，我也还是非常焦急地想知道谈话的内容。

对照 · 第 1635 组

The two women shut themselves in a parlour and I took up my position.

那两个女人把自己关在一间小客厅里，我也选好了偷听的位置。

对照 · 第 1636 组

'Well?' asked Marguerite.

怎么样？玛格丽特问。

对照 · 第 1637 组

'Well now, I saw the Duke.'

是这样，我见过公爵了。

对照 · 第 1638 组

'What did he say?'

他怎么说？

对照 · 第 1639 组

'He said he was quite ready to forgive that first scene, but he'd found out that you were living openly with Monsieur Armand Duval. He couldn't forgive that. "If Marguerite leaves this young man," he told me, "I'll give her anything she wants, as the past. If she doesn't, she can stop asking me for anything."

他说他愿意原谅那天的事，但是他已经知道您公开和阿尔芒迪瓦尔先生同居了。他不会原谅这件事的。如果玛格丽特离开这个年轻人，他告诉我说，我就像过去一样，满足她的一切要求。如果她不，她就别想再从我这里得到任何东西。

对照 · 第 1640 组

'What did you say to that?'

您是怎么回答他的？

对照 · 第 1641 组

'I said I'd pass on his decision, and I promised I'd make you see sense. Just think, dear girl, of the niche you'll be losing. Armand will never be able to make it up to you. He loves you with all his soul, but he doesn't have the money to pay for everything you need, and some day he's bound to leave you —when it'll be too late, and the Duke won't want to lend any more helping hands. 我说我会把他的决定告诉您，而且我会尽力让您明白事理。亲爱的孩子，考虑一下您会为此失去的地位吧。这是阿尔芒永远无法给您的。他的确全身心地爱着您，但他没有足够的钱来满足您的所有需要，他总有一天是要离开您的，但那时就太晚了，公爵不会再想帮助您了。您需要我去跟阿尔芒谈谈吗？

对照 · 第 1642 组

Marguerite seemed to be thinking, for she did not reply . My heart beat violently as I waited for her answer.

玛格丽特似乎在考虑，因为她一直没有回答。等待她的答案的时候，我的心脏剧烈地跳动着。

对照 · 第 1643 组

'No,' she resumed, 'I shall not leave Armand, and I shan't hide myself away so that I can go on living with him. Madness it may be, but I love him, there it is! And anyway, he's got into the habit of loving me without anything standing in his way. It would be much too painful for him to have to leave me for even an hour a day. Besides, I haven't got so much time to live that I can afford to make myself miserable just to please an old man: the very sight of him makes me feel old. Let him keep his money. I'll manage without.'

不，她接着说，我绝不离开阿尔芒，我也不会再隐瞒我和他的同居生活。这样做可能很傻，但我确实爱他，就是这样！况且，他早已习惯没有任何阻碍地爱我了。要他离开我，对他来说太痛苦了，哪怕一天只离开一小时。再说，我也活不了多久了，不想再委屈自己去讨好一个老头，每次一见到他，我甚至觉得自己都变老了。让他把钱留着吧。没他的钱我也能活。

对照 · 第 1644 组

'But what will you do?'

但是您以后怎么办呢？

对照 · 第 1645 组

'I have no idea.'

我不知道。

对照 · 第 1646 组

Prudence was probably about to reply to this, but I burst in, ran across to Marguerite and threw myself at her feet, covering her hands with the tears which the joy of being loved made me shed.

普鲁登斯可能还想说什么，但我已经冲了进去，跑过去扑倒在玛格丽特脚边，眼泪沾湿了她的双手，这眼泪因被爱的喜悦而流。

对照 · 第 1647 组

'My life is yours, Marguerite. You don't need this man: am I not here? How could I ever desert you? How could I ever repay the happiness you give me? Away with all constraints, dearest Marguerite! We love each other! What does the rest matter?'

我的生命是属于你的，玛格丽特。你不需要那个公爵，我不是在这儿吗？我怎么可能抛弃你呢？我怎么偿还得了你带给我的快乐呢？让一切约束都见鬼去吧，我最亲爱的玛格丽特！我们彼此相爱！其他的又有什么关系呢？

对照 · 第 1648 组

'Oh yes! I do love you, my Armand!' she murmured, circling my neck with both arms, 'I love you as I never believed I could love anybody. We will be happy, we'll live in peace, and I'll say goodbye forever to the old life I' m so ashamed of now. You'll never hold my past against me, will you?'

啊，是的！我爱你，我的阿尔芒！她双手紧紧搂着我的脖子轻声说道，我是这么爱你，爱到我都不相信自己会这样爱一个人。我们会快乐，我们会平静地生活，我会跟过去那种生活彻底告别，现在它令我感到羞耻。你一定不会因为我的过去而责备我的，是吗？

对照 · 第 1649 组

The tears dimmed my voice. The only answer I could give was to clasp Marguerite to my heart.

我哭得一句话都说不出来。我紧紧搂住玛格丽特，让她贴在我的胸口，这是我能给她的唯一答复。

对照 · 第 1650 组

'Come,' she said, turning to Prudence, her voice tinged with emotion, 'you can go and report this scene to the Duke and, while you're at it, tell him we don't need him.'

去吧，她转身对普鲁登斯说，声音因为激动而微微发颤，您就把这一幕讲给公爵听，讲的同时告诉他我们不再需要他了。

对照 · 第 1651–1700 组50 组
对照 · 第 1651 组

From that day on, the Duke was never mentioned again. Marguerite was no longer the girl I had met. She avoided anything which might have reminded me of the life she had been leading when I first made her acquaintance. Never did wife or sister show husband or brother such love, such consideration as she showed me. Her state of health left her open to sensation, and made her vulnerable to her feelings. She had broken with her women friends just as she had broken with her old ways; she controlled her language just as she curbed the old extravagance. Had you observed us leave the house for an outing in a delightful little boat I had bought, you would never have thought that this woman in a white dress, wearing a large straw hat and carrying on her arm a simple fur-lined silk coat which would protect her against the chill of the water, was the same Marguerite Gautier who, four months before, had attracted such attention with her extravagant ways and scandalous conduct.

从那天起，公爵再也没有被提起过。玛格丽特不再是我以前认识的那个姑娘了。凡是会让我想起我遇见她时她所过的生活的事，她都会尽量避免。她给我的爱和关心是任何一个妻子或姐妹都比不上的。她的身体状况使她容易激动，容易动感情。她不再跟她的那些女朋友来往，彻底放弃了过去的生活；她改变了谈吐气质，也不再像过去那样奢侈浪费。如果您看到我们一起出门，乘着我买的那条精巧的小船去游河，您肯定不会想到这个穿着白色连衣裙、头戴一顶大草帽、肩上披着一件用来抵挡河水寒气的普通的丝绸外衣的女人就是玛格丽特戈蒂埃，那个四个月之前还是以奢侈的生活方式和不体面的举止而闻名的女人。

对照 · 第 1652 组

Alas! We made haste to be happy, as though we had sensed that we should not be happy for long.

天哪！我们忙不迭地享受着这快乐的时光，仿佛已经察觉到这种好日子不会再持续多久一样。

对照 · 第 1653 组

We had not set foot in Paris for two months. No one had come down to see us, except Prudence and the same Julie Duprat whom I have already mentioned as the person in whose keeping Marguerite would later place the moving story now in my possession.

我们已经两个月没去过巴黎了。也没有人来看望我们，除了普鲁登斯和我跟您提到过的那个朱莉迪普拉，就是她替玛格丽特保存了现在在我手里的感人日记。

对照 · 第 1654 组

I spent whole day sat my mistress's feet. We would open the windows overlooking the garden and, as we watched the bright summer swoop down and open the flowers and settle under the trees, we would sit side by side and drink in this real, live world which neither Marguerite nor I had understood before.

我整日整夜地陪在我的爱人身边。我们会打开向着花园的窗子，看着盛夏一路袭来，使花朵绽放，然后在树下安顿下来；我们会肩并肩坐着陶醉在这真实、生机勃勃的世界中，不管是我还是玛格丽特，之前对这个世界都不曾有所了解。

对照 · 第 1655 组

She reacted with childish wonder to the most trivial things. There were days when she ran round the garden, like a girl of ten, chasing a butterfly or a dragonfly. This courtesan, who had made men spend more on flowers than would be needed to enable a whole family to live without a care, would sometimes sit on the lawn for an hour on end, examining the simple flower whose name she bore.

最细微的事情也会引起她孩子般的好奇心。有些日子，她就像个十岁的小女孩一样，在花园里奔跑、追赶一只蝴蝶或者蜻蜓。以前这个妓女让男人为她买花所花的钱甚至比维持一个家庭所需的钱还多，现在她有时会在草坪上坐上整整一个小时，出神地看着一朵跟她有着同样名字的普通的花朵。

对照 · 第 1656 组

It was at this time that she read Manon Lescaut so frequently. Many a time, I caught her writing in the margin of the book. And she always said that if a woman is truly in love, then that woman could never do what Manon did.

在这段时间里，她经常看《玛农莱斯科》。好几次我都看到她在书的空白处标注着什么。她总是对我说，如果一个女人真的恋爱了，她肯定不会像玛农那么做。

对照 · 第 1657 组

The Duke wrote to her two or three times. She recognized his writing and gave me his letters unread.

公爵给她写过两三封信。她认出是他的笔迹，看都不看地就交给了我。

对照 · 第 1658 组

On occasions, the wording of his letters brought tears to my eyes.

有几次，信里的话使我留下了眼泪。

对照 · 第 1659 组

He had thought that, by closing his purse to Marguerite, he could make her go back to him. But when he saw how ineffective his stratagem was, he was unable to carry it through. He had written, again asking her, as he had asked in the past, to allow him back to the fold, whatever conditions she chose to set for his return.

他原本以为，一旦切断玛格丽特的经济来源，她就会回到他身边。但当他看到这个方法没有半点儿作用的时候，他就再也忍不住了。他写信来再次请求她，就像过去请求她一样，请求她重新接纳他，无论她为此提出什么条件他都答应。

对照 · 第 1660 组

I thus had read his pressing, repeated letters and had torn them up, without telling Marguerite what they said or advising her to see the old man again—though a feeling of pity for the poor man's unhappiness did tempt me to do so. But I was afraid that she would see in my urging no more than a wish on my part to see the Duke resume his old visits, and thereby to see him assume responsibility once more for the household expenses. And above all, I feared that she would conclude that her love for me might lead to situations in which I would be capable of repudiating my responsibilities for her existence.

我看完这些反反复复、苦苦哀求的信之后，便把它们撕了，我没有告诉玛格丽特信的内容，也没有劝她回去再看看那个老人，虽然我对这个可怜老人的同情心的确诱使我这么做。我害怕玛格丽特会把我的劝告看成是我希望公爵继续来看望她，从而让他继续担负起所有的家庭开销。最重要的是，我害怕她会以为她对我的爱会导致这样的情况，即我能够拒绝履行我对她的责任。

对照 · 第 1661 组

The outcome was that the Duke, continuing to receive no answer, eventually stopped writing, and Marguerite and I continued our life together without a thought for the future.

结果是，公爵因为收不到回信，最终也就不再写信了，玛格丽特和我继续着我们的生活，根本不去考虑将来。

对照 · 第 1662 组

Chapter 18

第十八章

对照 · 第 1663 组

To tell you of our new life in any detail would be no easy matter. It was made up of a series of frivolous diversions which, though delightful to us, would be quite meaningless to anyone who heard me recount them. You know what it is to love a woman. You know how short the days seem and how loving the ease with which you let yourself drift towards the morrow. You are acquainted with that general neglect of things which is bred of violent, trusting, requited love. Any mortal being who is not the woman you love seems superfluous to creation. You regret having tossed pieces of your heart to other women, and you cannot imagine the prospect of ever holding a hand which is not the hand that you now hold clasped in yours. Your brain will entertain neither work nor memories, nor anything which might divert it from the one thought with which it is endlessly regaled. Each day you discover some new attraction in your mistress, some unknown sensual delight.

要把我们的新生活详详细细地告诉您可不是件容易的事。它由一系列琐碎的消遣组成，尽管对我们来说充满了快乐，但对听故事的人来说却是没有意义的。您知道爱一个女人是怎样一回事。您知道那些白天显得多么短暂，第二天清晨那么轻易地到来又是多么令人欢喜。您也知道那种相互分享、相互信赖的热烈爱情，可以让人忘掉一切。除了所爱的女人，其他任何人似乎都与您毫不相干。您后悔曾经喜欢过其他女人，甚至无法想象除了现在紧握着的这只手，您还会握住其他人的手。您的头脑不再考虑工作，也不再回忆过去，心里只有一个念头，那个给大脑带来无尽欢乐的念头，其他的任何想法都没法动摇它。每天您都能在情人身上发现一点儿新的魅力和一种未知的快感。

对照 · 第 1664 组

Life is no more than the repeated fulfilling of a permanent desire. The soul is merely the vestal handmaid whose task is to keep the sacred flame of love burning.

人生不过是在不断满足一种永恒的欲望。灵魂不过是维持爱情圣火不灭的贞洁圣女。

对照 · 第 1665 组

Often, after dark, we would go and sit in the little wood which overlooked the house. There we listened to the happy song of evening as we both thought of the approaching moment which would leave us in each other's arms till morning. At other times, we would stay in bed all day and not let even the sun into our bedroom. The curtains would be tightly drawn, and for us the world outside momentarily stopped turning. Nanine alone was authorized to open our door, but only to bring us our meals—and even so we ate them without getting up, and interrupted them constantly with laughter and all kinds of foolishness. And then would follow a few moments of sleep, for, retreating completely into our love, we were like two persistent divers who return to the surface only to take breath.

到了晚上，我们经常坐在那片可以俯瞰到我们房子的小树林里。我们倾听着夜晚快乐的歌曲，想着在那个即将到来的时刻我们又可以相互拥抱着直到清晨。有些时候，我们会整日地躺在床上，甚至不让阳光照进我们的卧室来。窗帘紧闭着，对我们而言，外面的一切都暂时停止了运转。只有纳尼娜可以打开我们的房门，但也只是送饭给我们，即使这样，我们也不起来而是在床上吃，还不时被嬉笑和各种蠢事打断。接着我们就再小睡一会儿，因为我们深深地陷入这样的爱情中，就像两个执着的潜水员，只有在换气时才会浮出水面。

对照 · 第 1666 组

However, I would catch Marguerite looking sad, and sometimes there were tears in her eyes. I would ask what was the reason for her sudden dejection and she would answer: This love of ours, my dearest Armand, is no ordinary love. You love me as though I'd never belonged to anyone else, and I tremble for fear that with time, regretting that you ever loved me and turning my past into a crime to hold against me, you might force me to resume the life from which you took me. Remember this: now that I've tasted a new kind of life, I should die if I had to take up the old one. So tell me you'll never leave me.'

但有好几次，我都看到玛格丽特很难过的样子，有时甚至眼里含着眼泪。我问她为什么忽然这么伤心，她回答说：我们的爱情不是普通的爱情，我最亲爱的阿尔芒。你那么爱我，就像我从未失身于别人一样，但我很害怕总有一天，您会后悔您爱过我，把我的过去当作一种罪恶来指责我，逼迫我不得不回到您帮我脱离掉的之前的那种生活。请记住这点：现在我已经尝到新生活的滋味，如果再回到过去，我肯定会死的。所以告诉我，你永远不会离开我。

对照 · 第 1667 组

'I swear it!'

我向你发誓！

对照 · 第 1668 组

At this, she would stare at me, as though she could read in my eyes whether my oath was sincere. Then she would throw herself into my arms and, burying her head in my chest, say: It's just that you have no idea how much I love you!'

听到这句话，她会盯着我看，似乎想从我的眼里看出这誓言到底是不是真的。然后她会扑到我怀里，把头埋在我的胸口，对我说：只因你不知道我有多么爱你！

对照 · 第 1669 组

One evening, we were leaning over the balcony outside our window. We gazed at the moon struggling to rise from its bed of clouds. We listened to the noise of the wind as it shook the trees. We held hands, and had not spoken for a good quarter of an hour when Marguerite said: Winter's coming. Would you like us to go away?'

一天晚上，我们倚在窗外的阳台上。我们凝望月亮，它正从云做的床上挣扎着起来。我们倾听着风摇动树木时发出的沙沙声。我们手握着手，大概有一刻钟没有说话，玛格丽特突然说：冬天就要来了。我们离开这儿吧，你说好吗？

对照 · 第 1670 组

'Where would we go?'

我们去哪里呢？

对照 · 第 1671 组

'Italy.'

意大利。

对照 · 第 1672 组

'Are you bored here?'

你觉得在这儿呆腻了？

对照 · 第 1673 组

'I'm afraid of winter. And I'm even more afraid of our going back to Paris.'

我害怕冬天。我更害怕回到巴黎去。

对照 · 第 1674 组

'Why?'

为什么呢？

对照 · 第 1675 组

'Lots of reasons.'

原因很多。

对照 · 第 1676 组

And she went on quickly, without explaining the reasons for her fears: Do you want to leave this place? I'll sell everything I have. We'll go and live far away. There'll be nothing left of the person I used to be. No one will know who I am. Would you like that?'

她没有告诉我她害怕的原因，只是立刻接着说：你想要离开这儿吗？我会把我的财产全都卖掉。我们会离开这儿，住得远远的。在那儿不会留下任何我过去的痕迹。没有人知道我是谁。你觉得好吗？

对照 · 第 1677 组

'We'll go, if that's what you want, Marguerite. Let's travel,' I said, 'but why the need to sell things you'll be glad to have when we get back? I haven't got enough money to accept a sacrifice like that, but I do have enough for us to travel in style for five or six months, if you fancy the idea at all.'

如果你想去的话，我们就去，玛格丽特。我们去那旅行，我说，但你为什么要把东西都卖掉呢？你回来时看到这些东西，还是会很开心的啊。我没有足够的钱来接受你这样的牺牲，但我的钱足以支撑我们作一次五六个月的旅行，只要你喜欢的话。

对照 · 第 1678 组

'If that's the way of it, no,' she continued, leaving the window and moving to the sofa in the dark shadow of the bedroom. What's the point of going all that way to spend money? I cost you enough here as it is.'

如果是这样的话，我们还是不要去了，她边说边离开窗户，坐到了房间暗处的沙发里。到那里去花钱有什么意义呢？我在这儿已经花了你不少钱了。

对照 · 第 1679 组

'That sounds like a reproach, Marguerite. You're being ungracious.'

这听起来像是埋怨啊，玛格丽特。这样可不好。

对照 · 第 1680 组

'Forgive me, my dear,' she said, holding out her hand to me, 'this stormy weather makes me irritable. I'm not saying what I mean.'

原谅我，亲爱的，她伸出手对我说，这种暴风雨天气使我变得急躁。我并不是那个意思。

对照 · 第 1681 组

And, after kissing me, she sat for a long time, lost in thought.

她吻了吻我，又在那里坐了好久，陷入了沉思。

对照 · 第 1682 组

Scenes like this occurred on several occasions and, though I remained ignorant as to their cause, I nevertheless sensed in Marguerite a feeling of anxiety for the future. It was not that she could have any doubts about my love for her, for it grew deeper with each passing day. And yet I ofter saw that she was sad, though she never explained why she was sad other than by alleging some physical reason.

这样的情况出现了好几次，尽管我仍不知道其中的原因，但我却感觉到了玛格丽特对未来的忧虑。她并不是怀疑我对她的爱，因为在过去的日子里，我对她的爱与日俱增。但是我却常常看到她一脸忧愁，尽管她从不向我解释她忧愁的原因，只是声称身体不舒服。

对照 · 第 1683 组

Fearing that she would weary of too monotonous a life, I suggested that we might return to Paris, but she invariably rejected the suggestion, and assured me that she could not be as happy anywhere as she was in the country.

我担心她对这种单调的生活厌烦了，就建议我们可以回巴黎去，但她还是一样拒绝了，还说再也没有什么地方会比这个乡下更令她快乐。

对照 · 第 1684 组

Prudence made only rare visits now. On the other hand, she wrote a number of letters which I never asked to see, although each one left Marguerite deeply preoccupied. I did not know what to make of it.

普鲁登斯现在也不怎么来了。但她经常写信过来，我从未要求看那些信，尽管玛格丽特总会被它们弄得心事重重。我不知道为什么。

对照 · 第 1685 组

One day, Marguerite remained in her room. I entered. She was writing.

有一天，玛格丽特呆在自己的房间里。我进去了。她正在写信。

对照 · 第 1686 组

'Who are you writing to?' I asked her.

在给谁写信？我问她。

对照 · 第 1687 组

'Prudence. Do you want me to read out what I've written?'

普鲁登斯。要不要我把信读给你听？

对照 · 第 1688 组

I had a profound distaste for anything that could seem like suspiciousness. So I answered Marguerite saying that there was no need for me to know what she was writing. And yet, I was sure of it, that letter would have acquainted me with the real reason for her fits of sadness.

对任何猜忌的行为我都讨厌至极。因此，我回答玛格丽特说我不需要知道她写些什么。但是，我可以断定这封信可以告诉我玛格丽特阵阵忧愁的真正原因。

对照 · 第 1689 组

The next day, the weather was superb. Marguerite suggested that we might take a boat out on the river and visit the le de Croissy. She seemed in the best of spirits. It was five o'clock by the time we got back.

第二天，天气特别好。玛格丽特提议我们乘船去克鲁瓦西岛玩。她看起来心情特别好。我们回到家时已经五点钟了。

对照 · 第 1690 组

'Madame Duvernoy came,' said Nanine as soon as she saw us come in.

迪韦努瓦太太来过。纳尼娜一看到我们进门就说。

对照 · 第 1691 组

'Did she go away again?' asked Marguerite.

她走了吗？玛格丽特问。

对照 · 第 1692 组

是的，坐夫人的马车走的。她说这没关系。

对照 · 第 1693 组

很好。玛格丽特紧接着说。快点开饭吧。

对照 · 第 1694 组

Two days later, there was a letter from Prudence, and for the next fortnight Marguerite seemed to have done with her mysterious sad moods, for which she never stopped asking me to forgive her now that they had ceased.

两天之后，普鲁登斯来了一封信，之后的两个星期，玛格丽特似乎也告别了她那神秘的忧伤情绪，而且还不断地要求我为这件事原谅她。

对照 · 第 1695 组

However, the carriage did not come back.

然而，马车却再也没有回来。

对照 · 第 1696 组

'How is it that Prudence hasn't returned your brougham?' I asked one day.

普鲁登斯为什么还不把马车还给你呢？有天我问她。

对照 · 第 1697 组

One of the horses is sick, and the carriage needs some repairs. It's better for all that to be done while we are still here where we don't need a carriage, than to wait until we get back to Paris.'

有匹马病了，而且马车也需要修理了。我们在这里也用不到马车，现在就把它修理好，总比等到我们回巴黎之后再修好。

对照 · 第 1698 组

Prudence came down to see us a few days after this and confirmed what Marguerite had told me.

过了几天，普鲁登斯来看我们，向我证实了之前玛格丽特的话。

The two women went for a stroll by themselves in the garden, and when I joined them they changed the subject they had been discussing.

两个女人在花园中散步，当我走近她们时，她们马上转变了刚才的话题。

那天晚上，普鲁登斯临走前抱怨天气太冷，把玛格丽特的开司米披肩借走了。

之后一个月，玛格丽特比过去都要高兴，也更加爱我了。

All this puzzled me in spite of myself and, since I knew in which drawer Marguerite kept Prudence's letters, I took advantage of a moment when she was at the bottom of the garden, hurried to the drawer and tried to open it. But it was no use: it was double-locked.

然而，马车没有回来，开司米披肩也再没送回来。我不由得开始对这一切产生怀疑，我知道玛格丽特把普鲁登斯的信都放在哪个抽屉里，于是，我利用玛格丽特在花园的一小会儿时间，匆忙跑到这个抽屉跟前，想打开看看。但一点用都没有，它被上了两层锁。

对照 · 第 1703 组

我又翻了她之前存放首饰和钻石的抽屉。我不费力气就打开了它们，但那些首饰盒却都消失了自然里面的首饰也不见了。

一阵恐慌涌上我的心头。

我很想去问问玛格丽特这些东西到底是怎么不见的。但我知道她是肯定不会告诉我实情的。

对照 · 第 1706 组

对照 · 第 1725 组

'There's thirty thousand francs or so outstanding. I told you, dear, didn't I? You just wouldn't believe me. Well then, are you convinced now? The upholsterer, who had the Duke as her guarantor, was shown the door when he went to see the Duke who wrote him a letter the next day saying that he wouldn't lift a finger for Mademoiselle Gautier. The man wanted money. He was given something on account—the few thousand francs I asked you for. Then some kind souls let him know that his non-paying customer had been dropped by the Duke and was living with some young man who had no money. The other creditors were likewise told. She had to pay of course, and to avoid asking you for money, she sold her horses and her Indian shawls and pawned her jewels. Do you want the buyers' receipts and the pawn tickets?'

欠了三万法郎左右。我告诉过您，亲爱的，不是吗？您只是不相信我。现在您相信了吧？那个地毯商，之前有公爵替她做担保人，去找公爵时吃了闭门羹，第二天公爵给他写信说他再也不会为玛格丽特戈蒂埃付一个铜板了。那个人来要钱。我们只好分期付给他，我之前从您这里要走的那几千法郎就是给他的。后来，他从一些好心人那里听说了公爵已经不再理会他的债务人了，她现在跟一个身无分文的年轻人住在一起。其他债主也同样知道了这件事。他们也来要债，还查封了她的一些财产。玛格丽特想把东西都卖了，但已经太晚了，而且，我也不赞成这个做法。她不得不还债，而她又不想找您要钱，因此她卖掉了她的马和开司米披肩，当掉了她的珠宝。您想看那些收据和当票吗？

对照 · 第 1726 组

对照 · 第 1727 组

'Do you imagine,' she continued, as persistent as any woman who is entitled to say: 'I was right!', 'do you imagine that it's enough to love each other and go off to the country and live some dreamy, rustic life? Oh no, my dear. Alongside the ideal life, there's the necessities to think of, and the purest designs are earthbound, secured by threads which, ludicrous though they may be, are made of steel and cannot be easily snapped. If Marguerite hasn't deceived you twenty times and more it's because she has an exceptional nature. It's not her fault if I advised her to do so, because it grieved me to see the poor girl strip herself of everything. And she wouldn't have anything to do with it! She told me she loved you and wouldn't deceive you for anything. All that's very nice, very poetic, but it's not coin you can pay off creditors with. And now she's reached the stage where she won't get away with it unless she comes up with, let me say it again, thirty thousand francs.'

您真的以为，她接着说，脸上的表情跟那些洋洋得意地宣布着看吧，我说对了的女人一模一样，您真的以为，你们只要相爱，然后躲到乡下去就能过那种梦一般的田园生活了吗？噢，不是的，我亲爱的。除了这种理想生活，我们还要考虑现实，那些最纯洁的欲望有时不得不被一些看来可笑，但却被钢铁般难以折断的绳索束缚。如果玛格丽特从来不骗您，那是因为她有着与众不同的性格。我劝她这么做，她并没有做错，因为我真不忍心看这个可怜的姑娘失去一切。但她却不听我的话！她告诉我说她爱您，她绝不会欺骗您。这些听起来那么美好，那么诗情画意，但这不能替她还债。现在到了无法逃脱的地步，除非凑齐，我再告诉您一次，三万法郎。

没关系。我会弄到钱的。

对照 · 第 1731 组

'Now that would be really clever. You'll fall out with your father, tie up your allowance and, anyway, you can't just come up with thirty thousand francs from one day to the next. 这主意可真不错啊。您会跟您父亲闹翻，经济来源也会被冻结，无论如何，您也没办法在一两天内弄到三万法郎。听我的话吧，我亲爱的阿尔芒，我比您更了解女人。别这么做，这种做法太愚蠢了，您总有一天会后悔的。理智点儿吧。我并不是要您离开玛格丽特，您只要像初夏时那样跟她生活在一起就行了。让她自己来想办法解决问题。公爵会慢慢改变主意的。

对照 · 第 1733 组

Prudence was right, cruelly right.

普鲁登斯是对的，对得残酷。

对照 · 第 1734 组

'That's how it is,' she continued, shutting away the papers she had just shown me. Otherwise, they'd put a bit to one side and, by the time they're thirty, they'd be able to afford the luxury of taking a lover who pays nothing. If only I'd known once what I know now! Within a fortnight, she'll take on Count de N, she'll put some money by this winter, and then next summer you can pick up where you left off. That's how it's done, my dear!'

就是这么回事，她收起之前给我看过的那些票据，接着说道。妓女总期盼着周围有男人来爱她，但她们却从没想到，自己有一天也会爱上别人。不然，她们就会开始攒钱，等到三十岁的时候，就能负担得起和一个一分钱不给的情人的生活了。要是我能早点儿明白这点就好了！只不过是顺带提一句。您跟玛格丽特什么也别说，只把她带回巴黎就好了。您已经跟她单独呆了四五个月了，这已经很不错了。您所需要做的就是睁一只眼闭一只眼。两周之内，她就会再接待

In he first three letters, my father expressed his concern for my silence and asked the reason for it. In he last, he made it clear that he had been informed of my changed way of life, and announced his arrival in the very near future.

我一向很尊敬我的父亲，始终对他怀有很深的感情。因此我回信说，我之所以不回信是因为我之前去旅行了，我又请他提前把到达的日期告诉我，以便我去接他。

对照 · 第 1777 组

In affairs like ours, as long as the woman has something of her self- respect left, she must shoulder any number of sacrifices herself rather than ask her lover for money and in so doing taint her love with mercenary motives. You love me, I know you do, but you have no idea just how weak are the ties that bind the love men have for girls like me. Who knows? Perhaps one day, when you were short of money or feeling annoyed, you'd have come round to thinking that our affair was a carefully worked-out plot! Prudence talks too much. I didn't need those horses! I've saved myself money by selling them: I can manage without, and now I don't have to spend anything on them. As long as you love me, that's all I ask. And you can love me just as much without horse and shawls and diamonds.'

像我们这样的关系，只要这个女人还有一点点儿的自尊心，她就必须承担任何牺牲，而不向她的情人要钱，否则她的爱情就跟卖淫没什么区别。你爱我，这点我完全明白，但你却不知道，男人对我这种女人的爱的约束力有多么脆弱。谁又知道呢？也许你哪天缺钱了，或者心情不好，你就会把我们的爱情当作一场精心策划的阴谋。普鲁登斯总喜欢多嘴。我根本不需要那些马！把它们卖掉后我省了不少钱：没有这些马我也能过日子，而且也不用再在它们身上花钱。只要你爱我，这是我唯一要求的事情。即使没有那些马、披肩和首饰，你也会一样爱我。

Nor can I stand the thought that you should ever regret living with me, even for a moment. A few days from now, your horses, your diamonds and your shawls will be returned to you. You need them as much as life needs air. It may be ridiculous, but I'd rather have you lavish than frugal.'

亲爱的姑娘，因为，我不想你因为对我的感情而变得贫穷，哪怕少一件珠宝都不行。跟你一样，我不希望你在遇到困难或心情不好时会想到，如果你跟别人在一起的话，就不会经历这些糟糕的事了。我也无法忍受你因为跟我在一起而感到后悔，哪怕一分钟也不行。几天之后，你的马、首饰和披肩都会回到你身边。你需要这些东西，就像生命需要空气一样。这听起来也许很可笑，但我宁愿你过得奢侈些，也比节俭来得好。

如果你真的爱我，就让我用我自己的方式来爱你。但你一直把我当成那种奢侈成性的姑娘，那种你不得不花钱养活的姑娘。你羞于接受我爱你的证据。在你心里，你一直在想哪天总会离开我，一直在小心地用不安来掩饰你的疑心。你是对的，我亲爱的，只是我以前期待的太多了。

对照 · 第 1788 组

'You. You won't take me into your confidence by saying exactly where you stand, and you're vain enough to want to keep me in my place. You don't consider that my feelings are sufficiently disinterested to want to share that money you have with me so that we could live happily together. No, you'd sooner ruin yourself. A slave to a stupid prejudice, that's what you are. Do you really think I compare a carriage and bits of jewelry with your love? Do you imagine I think happiness consists of those empty pleasures which people make do with when they've got nothing to love, but which seem so unimportant when they have? You'll pay my debts, you' ll sign away all you have and you'll be my keeper! And how long will that last? Two or three months — and then it'll be too late to start the life I'm offering you, for then you'd be kept by me, and that's something which no self-respecting man could accept. Whereas at the moment, you've got eight or ten thousand francs a year on which we can manage. I'll sell everything I don' t need, and by investing the proceeds I'd have a steady two thousand a year. We'll rent a nice little apartment and live there together. In summer, we'll come down to the country, not to a house like this, but to something smaller, just big enough for two. You've no ties, I'm free, and we're young. For heaven's sake, Armand, don't make me go back to the life I had to lead once!'

是你啊。你不肯对我诉说心事，不告诉我你的处境，而且要我像过去那样生活，以此来满足自己的虚荣心。你想让我继续过去那种奢侈的生活，你想继续维持我们道德上的距离。你就是这样。你不认为我会愿意和你分享你那点钱来一起幸福地生活。不，你宁愿让自己倾家荡产。被愚蠢的成见所奴役，那就是你。你真以为那些马车和首饰可以跟你的爱相比吗？你知道吗？我认为，人们在没有爱情的时候才会把空虚的玩乐当幸福，而他们一旦拥有了爱情，这些东西就变得毫无价值了。你要替我还债，你把自己的钱都花光，然后来养我！这又能维持多久呢？两三个月？到那时再想按我说的方法去生活就太晚了，因为到那时你就要被我养着，这是任何一个有自尊心的男人都不愿接受的。而现在，你每年有八千到一万法郎的收入，我们可以靠这笔钱过下去。我把用不到的东西都卖掉，然后用卖得的钱来投资，这样我们每年又会有两千利弗尔的稳定收入。我们可以租一套漂亮的小公寓，一起在那生活。夏天的时候，我们可以到乡下玩，我们不要现在这种房子，只要一栋足够我们两人住的小房子就行了。你没有牵绊，我也自由自在，而且我们年纪还轻。看在老天的份上，阿尔芒，别再让我回到从前那种不得不过的日子里去了。

我马上为我之后的生活做好了打算。我把我的财产重新做了安排，把我从我母亲的遗产中获得的那笔收入给了玛格丽特，虽然这还远远无法报答她为我做出的牺牲。

对照 · 第 1800 组

The money in question derived from a mortgage of sixty thousand francs on a house which I had never even seen. All I knew was that each quarter, my father's solicitor, an old family friend, handed over seven hundred and fifty francs against my signature.

这笔钱来自一栋六万法郎的房子的抵押金，我从没见过这栋房子。我只知道每一个季度，我父亲的公证人我们家的一个老朋友都会凭我的收据给我七百五十法郎。
